EXTERNAL TOOLS PRODUCE C, AND THAT IS NOT AN ARGUMENT FOR IT. Mutalyzer and VariantValidator both emit C; biocommons `hgvs` cannot represent the row at all, its grammar having no allele rule. The filer's own words on the merge are "shortcuts that merge deletion-insertions" (#1430) and "these were over-merged by mutalyzer" (#182) — Mutalyzer is a method to emulate where it is deterministic and confluent, never a spec oracle. Agreement between two tools that over-merge is not evidence that the merge is the recommended form.

3'-MAXIMALITY DOES NOT DECIDE THIS, AND AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS REASONING CLAIMED IT DID. Recorded so no later reader reintroduces the error: all three forms are 3'-maximal as written. A's `1001009_1001010del` deletes `GC` as one unit, and shifting it 3' by one would require `ref[1001009] == ref[1001011]`, i.e. `G == C`, which fails. `general.md:40` — "for all descriptions, the most 3' position possible of the reference sequence is arbitrarily assigned to have been changed" — does not discriminate between A, B and C. An earlier revision asserted that A "is not 3'-maximal" and rested the argument on that; it is withdrawn. What decides this is how the edit set is PARTITIONED into members, not where a given member sits.

THE COST, MEASURED, AND OVER-STATED ON PURPOSE. Converging on A moves output: `live` emits B for the second spelling today. The eventual fix is not implemented, so the blast radius is quoted from a PROXY — the sequence-first arm, which produces the decided form on this record's case. Over `examples/dump_normalized_corpus.rs`'s 78,298-row synthetic corpus, `FERRO_PARTITION=live` against `=canonical` moves 9,567 rows (12.2%), 6,764 of them previously fixed points; divergent equivalence classes fall from 5,366 to 3,392 (3': 2,697 to 1,710; 5': 2,669 to 1,682), with ZERO classes newly divergent and ZERO rows newly denoting different bases. That corpus is enriched for churn-prone shapes, so the rate is within those shape families and not a library-wide figure, and swapping the whole partitioner is broader than a fix scoped to this record — read it as a ceiling. Disclosure is what the ruling owes: rule 7 of the ruleset, and the filer's own instruction that the project "should just make sure to call instability out as a 'breaking' change" (#1235).

AND THE QUESTION HAS AN INSERTION-SIDE INSTANCE. `contiguous-insertion-split-by-a-blocked-derivation` records the same phenomenon reached from insertions rather than deletions: inside a tandem tract one contiguous 3 nt insertion can be spelled as an `ins` at one junction plus a `dup` inserting at the next, which presents a separation of 1 that the variant does not have. That record settles only the scope of `general.md:33` on such a locus. It does not settle which partition is canonical in general, which is the residue named in the paragraph below.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT SETTLE. Which partition is canonical for every `(reference, sequence)` pair. `canonical-form-choice-when-both-legal` decides the METHOD — re-derive from the resulting sequence, subject to the spec's explicit tie-breaks — and leaves the choice among equally-minimal partitions to `adjudication-precedence-order`. What this record settles is the narrower question it was opened on, and the one `:33` could not answer: the separation is read off the re-derived partition, which makes `general.md:33` evaluable and confluent. Nothing here licenses reading a separation off written positions in any other class, and nothing here adjudicates a contest whose competing forms are all reached by re-derivation.

MEASURED — THIS RECORD SUPERSEDED A COMMITTED GUARD, 2026-08-13. Recorded here because a deleted test leaves no trace otherwise, and because the guard was not obviously wrong: it was argued, cited and passing.

WHAT WAS DELETED. `tests/it/reported_confluence_pairs.rs::the_1420_v2_pair_does_not_converge_by_re_derivation`, which asserted that `TEMPLATE:g.[37dup;41del]` must NOT normalize to `TEMPLATE:g.[38T>A;40_41delinsTG]`. Its stated ground was that the two members "are separated by three unchanged nucleotides - 38, 39 and 40", so `general.md:33` kept them individual.

WHY IT WAS WRONG, AND IT IS EXACTLY THIS RECORD'S SUBJECT. Those three bases are what THE INPUT'S SPELLING leaves between members written at 37 and 41. This record holds that the separation `general.md:33` keys on is read off the partition RE-DERIVED FROM THE RESULTING SEQUENCE, never off the spelling the input happened to use. Read that way the output is TWO MEMBERS AT SEPARATION ONE, DESCRIBED INDIVIDUALLY across the unchanged 39 - which is what `:33` asks for. NOTHING MERGED. The guard's own phrase, that the census would rise "purely because the merge-across-unchanged-bases veto stopped firing", names an event that did not occur; that phrase is what made the guard look load-bearing, and it is the sentence to distrust if this shape recurs.

THE DESTINATION IS INDEPENDENTLY SPEC-CORRECT, on the argument already committed beside the row in `reported_partition_verdicts`: `general.md:55` ranks substitution above duplication, so the change at 38 may not be spelled as a duplication; `delins.md:17` keeps the two runs individual across the unchanged 39. Mutalyzer merges this shape and does not get a vote, under `adjudication-precedence-order`'s spec-explicit > Mutalyzer.

WHAT CARRIED THE MOVE, AND THE SCOPE CHECK IT PASSES. `derivation-may-not-be-bounded-by-the-inputs-spelling` (#1616) deleted the input-relative weight bound, so the block is re-derived instead of handed back. That record's scope paragraph warns its deletion "does not license the merges the comparand happened to be blocking". Respected: this is not a merge, by the reading above.

CONSEQUENCES, MEASURED BOTH DIRECTIONS. `reported_confluence_pairs`' converging-pair census rises 3 -> 9 of 9, with 0 still split, in BOTH directions (measured separately, not assumed to match). `reported_partition_verdicts`' `OPEN_GAPS` falls 12 -> 11 and the `1420-v2/cis` verdict flips to Canonical; the stored spelling that moved is `TEMPLATE:g.[37dup;41del]` -> `TEMPLATE:g.[38T>A;40_41delinsTG]`, so a consumer holding the reported form gets the re-derived one back.

AND AN OPEN CONSEQUENCE THIS RULING DOES NOT SETTLE. `reported_partition_verdicts` models a reported pair as ONE canonical spelling and ONE gap, and asserts the two verdicts differ. Five of the nine pairs now reach their wanted form from BOTH spellings (1420-v2, 1420-v3, 1420-v4, 1421-n1, 1421-n3), which that model cannot express - and `each_pair_reaches_its_canonical_form_from_exactly_one_spelling` says in its own doc that it is written to fail the day this happens, so that progress is as loud as regression. It is failing as designed. Only the 1420-v2 row is decided here; the model change, and the other four pairs, are NOT.

MEASURED — THE EIGHT CODING-AXIS MERGES, DERIVED RATHER THAN LABELLED, 2026-08-13. #1710's instrument `coding_axis_separation_two_or_more_merges` reads 0 -> 8 in BOTH directions on #1616 (of 997 evaluated). The row ids carry `sep2`/`sep3`/`sep8`, but those are THE GENERATOR'S CONSTRUCTION, and this record forbids reading separation off anything but the re-derived partition. Measured, so the labels are not taken on trust. MEASUREMENT ONLY — NO RULING IS MADE HERE.

| row | authored | derived (3') | span | deleted | retained | authored gaps | |---|---|---|---|---|---|---| | s01-c1-m3-all-del-p1-sep2 | `c.[9del;12del;15del]` | `c.9_15delinsGAGT` | 7 | 3 | 4 | 2, 2 | | s01-c1-m4-all-del-p2-sep2 | `c.[9_10del;13_14del;17_18del;21_22del]` | `c.9_22delinsACATTA` | 14 | 8 | 6 | 2, 2, 2 | | s01-c3m-m3-all-del-p4-sep3 | `c.[24_27del;31_34del;38_41del]` | `c.24_41delinsTTCGGT` | 18 | 12 | 6 | 3, 3 | | s01-c3m-m4-all-del-p4-sep3 | `c.[24_27del;31_34del;38_41del;45_48del]` | `c.24_48delinsTTCGGTCAT` | 25 | 16 | 9 | 3, 3, 3 | | s01-c1-m3-all-del-p2-sep8 | `c.[9_10del;19_20del;29_30del]` | `c.9_30delinsACGTATCCACGCGACT` | 22 | 6 | 16 | 8, 8 |

FINDING 1 — THE DERIVED PARTITION IS ONE MEMBER IN ALL EIGHT, SO IT HAS NO SEPARATIONS AT ALL. Read as this record requires, the separation count of every one of the eight outputs is ZERO, because a single member has nothing to be separated from. The `sepN` in each id is the AUTHORED separation and is accurate as such (2, 2, 3, 3, 8) — it is simply not the quantity this record says `general.md:33` counts.

FINDING 2 — THE PAYLOAD IS ENTIRELY RETAINED REFERENCE BASES. In all five designs `retained == payload length` exactly (4/4, 6/6, 6/6, 9/9, 16/16), and the sequence-preservation oracle reads 0, which forces the payload to BE the retained bases rather than merely to match in length. So NOTHING IS INSERTED by these merges: a split of pure deletions inserts nothing, and the merged spelling only re-supplies the bases the split had kept. That is the mechanism half of the argument that `delins.md:44-47`'s payload-COINCIDENCE never occurs here — there is no payload to coincide, only reference. RECORDED AS AN OBSERVATION, NOT AS A RULING: the argument rests on `delins-recommendation-reach-when-the-input-arrives-split` in its DECIDED form, and that record is UNDECIDED on `origin/main` with its decided form living in the open, conflicting PR #1760. Sound and currently unavailable; do not cite it as settled.

FINDING 3 — s01-c1-m3-all-del-p2-sep8 REALLY IS 8, AND STANDS APART. Its two retained runs are eight bases each, and retained (16) is more than twice what is deleted (6) — the merged form carries MORE reference than it removes. Whatever argument covers the 2s and 3s on a coincidence-scale reading does not obviously reach this one, and it should be argued separately rather than swept in with the other seven.

#### `separation-rule-force-modal-or-negation`

**Status:** decided

**The question.** Does the negation in “should be described individually and not as a ‘delins’” carry prohibition force of its own, making `general.md:33` a README rule 1 (Conformant, absolute), or does the modal grade the whole clause, making it rule 2 (Recommended form, best effort)?

**Governing clause.**

- `docs/recommendations/DNA/delins.md:81`
  > should preferably be described individually and not as a "delins" (unless they together affect one amino acid)

**Also cited.**

- `docs/recommendations/general.md:33`
  > two variants separated by one or more nucleotides should be described individually and **not** as a "delins"
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/duplication.md:18`
  > it **must** be described as a duplication and not as, e.g., an insertion
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/substitution.md:32`
  > the description <code class="invalid">c.[79G>T;80C>T]</code> is not correct
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/substitution.md:16`
  > two variants separated by one or more nucleotides should be described individually and not as a "delins" of the sequence affected

**The ruling.**

OPERATOR RULING, 2026-08-12 — DECIDED. `general.md:33` is README **rule 2** (Recommended form, best effort) in its entirety. Its negation does not carry prohibition force of its own. The general reading this record establishes, which is what makes it reusable beyond this clause: “and not Y” NAMES THE EXCLUDED ALTERNATIVE; IT DOES NOT GRADE THE CLAUSE. THE MODAL GRADES THE CLAUSE.

FIRST REASON, AND IT IS INTERNAL TO THE CLAUSE. The tempting reading splits it — “should be described individually” as rule 2, “and not as a ‘delins’” as rule 1. That is incoherent here, because the two halves are COMPLEMENTS: for two variants separated by one or more nucleotides, “described individually” and “described as a delins” exhaust the available forms. If the delins form were forbidden, the individual form would be the only permitted one — that is, MANDATORY — and the word “should” would be doing no work at all. A clause cannot grade its positive half as a preference while forbidding the sole alternative to it. The halves therefore share one force, and the modal supplies it.

SECOND REASON: THE SPEC PAIRS THE SAME CONSTRUCTION WITH BOTH MODALS. “and not” occurs 14 times in `docs/recommendations/` at spec checkout `6f85311`. Twelve take “should”; exactly two take “must” — `DNA/duplication.md:18` and `RNA/duplication.md:19`. Were the negation prohibitive on its own, that “must” would be redundant. It is not, and `duplication.md:18` shows why the two constructions differ: its alternatives are NOT exhaustive (“and not as, **e.g.**, an insertion”), so there “must X” and “not Y” are genuinely separate propositions and the modal does independent work. That is what a real prohibition looks like in this spec, and even there the force comes from the modal rather than from the negation.

THIRD REASON, AND THE DECISIVE ONE: THE SPEC RESTATES THIS VERY RULE AS A PREFERENCE. `DNA/delins.md:81`, inside a Q&A block — which `CLAUDE.md` records as adjudicative rather than colour — answers: “Yes, two variants separated by one or more nucleotides should **preferably** be described individually and not as a ‘delins’ (unless they together affect one amino acid).” Same rule, same “and not”, with “preferably” attached. “preferred” is named in README rule 2's own scope. The spec states the force directly.

FOURTH: EVERY RULE-1 MARKER IS ABSENT, CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED. The separation rule is stated five times across the spec — `general.md:33`, `DNA/substitution.md:16`, `DNA/delins.md:81`, `RNA/substitution.md:17`, `protein/delins.md:21` — and never once with “must”, “not allowed”, “can not be used”, or `class="invalid"`. `general.md` contains ZERO `class="invalid"` markup in the entire file. `checklist.md` does not enumerate the separation rule: grepping it for `separat|delins|individual` returns only unrelated items about recessive-phenotype combinations and about reporting numbers separately. So none of the five prohibition markers README rule 1 names, nor its `checklist.md` escape hatch, reaches this clause.

WHERE THE SPEC DOES DRAW THE PROHIBITION LINE, AND WHY THIS IS COHERENT RATHER THAN CONVENIENT. At separation ZERO the spec marks the split spelling `class="invalid"` and calls it “not correct” by name (`DNA/substitution.md:32`) — which is the ground `delins-adjacent-members-when-both-consume-reference` was already decided on. So the spec's own line is: separation zero is a prohibition, separation one or more is a preference. This record does not move that line; it reads it.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT LICENSE. Rule 2 is a rule. README states that a preference clause OUTRANKS MAINTAINER JUDGMENT, so ferro remains obliged, on best effort, to produce the individual form. What changes is only the CLASSIFICATION: an output merging across one or more unchanged nucleotides on a frameless axis is a rule-2 miss to be disclosed and pinned as a deviation, not a rule-1 violation that README rule 7 makes a bug. It therefore does not by itself block a release. Every negative guard measuring the deviation is to be KEPT and pinned at its TRUE value with a tripwire that fails when a listed row starts conforming — the `FERRO_IS_WRONG` shape — so a deviation cannot rot into an unnoticed permanent exemption. Re-pinning such a guard to zero to obtain a green build is the one move this record forbids.

WHAT WAS REFUTED, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT RE-DERIVED. Two readings were argued at length before this ruling and both are WITHDRAWN. (1) That the bolded “not” in `general.md:33` is prohibition force under README rule 1's “prose force rather than keyword casing” clause — refuted by the complement argument above and by `delins.md:81`. (2) That the clause splits, rule 2 on the positive half and rule 1 on the negative — refuted by the same complement argument; the split reading requires the alternatives NOT to exhaust the space, which is the `duplication.md:18` shape and not this one.

SCOPE. This record rules on the FORCE of the separation rule, and on the reading of “and not” generally. It does not decide which form ferro emits where several remain legal — that is `canonical-form-choice-when-both-legal`; nor the reach of the codon exception — that is `projection-codon-exception-is-decided-by-the-rendered-axis`; nor `delins.md:47`'s carve-out — that is `delins-payload-coincidence-carve-out-is-coding-dna-scoped`. Nor does it disturb `delins-merge-vs-individual-gap-two-or-more`, which scopes WHEN `:33` yields to `delins.md:44-47`; this record is about how strongly `:33` speaks when it does apply.

#### `unchanged-is-read-over-every-minimal-alignment`

**Status:** decided

**The question.** `general.md:33` keys on variants 'separated by one or more nucleotides' and `DNA/delins.md:16` on 'two or more consecutive nucleotides'. Both quantify over nucleotides being unchanged or changed, and neither names an alignment. Against WHICH alignment is a reference base judged unchanged — the position-wise correspondence available when the two sides are the same length, or the minimal-edit alignment?

**House choice.** This ruling is the project's own, made under `README.md` rule 5 (silent limb) where the recommendations do not decide. It cites no governing clause and must never be quoted as conformance.

**Considered and rejected.** Four readings were weighed and put aside, and the first is the one that matters because it is the reading this record replaced. THE POSITION-WISE READING — pair column i of the reference against column i of the observed. Rejected, but NOT because a clause forbids it: it has no less textual support than the minimal-alignment reading, and no more. It is rejected because it is not stable under the thing it is supposed to describe — on an equal-length block it reports changed columns that a cheaper alignment leaves untouched, so two spellings of one variant can present different separations and `general.md:34` then returns two answers for one variant. `rulings[derivation-may-not-be-bounded-by-the-inputs-spelling]` records four rows where exactly that happened. READING `general.md:34` AS ITSELF SUPPLYING THE PROCEDURE. Rejected on measurement rather than on argument, so the measurement is stated with the command that produced it and with the checkout it was run at. In the pinned submodule at `565b973`: `git grep -n -i -E 'minimal|minimum' -- docs/recommendations docs/background docs/consultation`. Over those three trees `cost` is 0, `parsimon*` 0, `edit distance` 0, `optimal` 0, `fewest` 0, and there are SEVEN `minimal`/`minimum` hits, all seven in `docs/recommendations/` and none in `docs/background/` or `docs/consultation/`. They fall into three groups, and the third is the one an earlier draft of this paragraph mis-filed: the extent of an unsequenced deletion or duplication (`uncertain.md:33`, `:159`); SNP-array probe bounds (`DNA/deletion.md:100`, `DNA/duplication.md:106`); and one notational character-economy note — 'the recommendations try to restrict the number of different characters used to a minimum' — repeated verbatim on all three axes (`DNA/insertion.md:106`, `RNA/insertion.md:62`, `protein/insertion.md:73`). That third group is the closest thing in the corpus to the concept being disclaimed and it is notational, not operational. None of the seven edits minimality, so the conclusion is unchanged; what changed is the arithmetic, which in a paragraph resting on measurement is not a detail. SEVEN IS NOT AN ARTEFACT OF HOW THE SCAN IS DRAWN: case-sensitive and case-insensitive agree, per-line and per-occurrence agree, adding word boundaries agrees (checked under `perl`, because `git grep -E` does not honour `\b` and silently returns 0 for it), and restricting to `*.md` agrees; the only `minim*` forms present anywhere in the three trees are `minimal` twice and `minimum` five times. The count is also unchanged from the `6f85311` checkout this paragraph was first measured at — all seven line anchors survived the bump to `565b973` unmoved. There is no definition of `unchanged` for a block differing at several positions, and no procedure of any kind for deriving a partition from two sequences. `:34` raises the question and does not answer it. PROVENANCE-BASED INDIVIDUATION, which appears to be the spec's own model — `DNA/delins.md:81-84` grounds the split-by-default rule in the two variants having 'been reported (or might occur) individually', and `DNA/complex.md:12` says HGVS describes 'the variant(s) detected'. Rejected as unavailable rather than as wrong: a normalizer holds a reference and an observed sequence and no report, so it cannot recover provenance. `rulings[separation-is-a-property-of-the-spelling-not-of-the-variant]` already records that deviation. COUNTER-EVIDENCE, RECORDED UNRESOLVED. `DNA/delins.md:44-47` is the corpus's only worked confrontation with alignment-derived splitting and it prefers the COARSER form, calling the split 'an alternative description' and recommending the spanning `delins` on two stated grounds; `:46` puts the word 'align' in the committee's own scare quotes; and `DNA/delins.md:86-89` records a second case where permission for a split spelling was WITHDRAWN. That is evidence against reading alignment-derived splitting as the spec's method, and it is not answered here. Two existing records scope `:47` away from the blocks this rule governs — `delins-payload-coincidence-carve-out-is-coding-dna-scoped` to the `c.` axis and `delins-merge-vs-individual-gap-two-or-more` to the net-deletion direction — so the passage does not currently reach them, but that is a consequence of those scopes and not a refutation of the counter-evidence. REVISITED 2026-08-15, AND BOTH SCOPES HOLD. `delins.md:86-89` is a CODING-axis, net-INSERTION, separation-ZERO case already ruled on by `delins-adjacent-members-when-both-consume-reference`, so it reaches neither scope; and it was in front of the operator when both were written, `c0742311` having brought it into the ledger 52 minutes before the axis scope landed. Widening the axis is blocked rather than merely unattractive: a frameless two-member split at separation one falls inside `spec_corpus::is_svd_wg010_shape`, which `guard_violations` pins at zero over 210 rows as a verdict on the rejected proposal. Widening the direction is near-vacuous, because `payload_embeds_within_budget` refuses a longer payload on its own terms independently of the direction gate. The counter-evidence above is therefore recorded as UNANSWERED-BUT-NOT-REACHING, which is a different thing from unexamined.

**Also cited.**

- `docs/recommendations/general.md:33`
  > two variants separated by one or more nucleotides should be described individually and **not** as a "delins"
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/delins.md:16`
  > changes involving two or more consecutive nucleotides are described as deletion/insertion (delins) variants
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/substitution.md:32`
  > changes involving two or more consecutive nucleotides are described as deletion-insertion (delins) so the description
- `docs/background/basics.md:38`
  > The recommendations for the description of sequence variants are designed to be **stable**, **meaningful**, **memorable**, and **unequivocal**

**The ruling.**

DECIDED 2026-08-07, while building the forced-unchanged-column detector for #1539/#1540; RECORDED HERE 2026-08-14, because it was never written down and the cost of that came due twice in one hour.

THE RULING. A reference base is unchanged iff it is matched in EVERY MINIMAL alignment of the block. The notion is COLUMN-based, not cell-based and not position-wise. `general.md:33`'s 'separated by one or more nucleotides' and `delins.md:16`'s 'two or more consecutive nucleotides' are both evaluated against that notion.

THE DISTINCTION THAT FORCED IT, from the 2026-08-07 work: `Dominators::matched_ref()` asks 'is one (ref, alt) CELL a Match on every minimal path?', while the clause asks 'is this reference BASE unchanged in every minimal alignment?'. On `GACA -> AGAT` the three cost-3 alignments (matched reference sets `{1,3}`, `{0,1}`, `{0,1}`, intersecting to `{1}`) all match ref offset 1, but to different alt offsets, so the cell-based notion sees nothing while the base is unchanged either way. That is a notion mismatch, not a bug: `dominators()` was checked against its own brute-force oracle and agrees with it. READ THAT CORROBORATION NARROWLY (#1959): the probe walks the DAG it is checking -- its cell set, its edges, its reachability -- and re-derives nothing from the two sequences, so it establishes that `dominators()` is consistent with edge-banned reachability OVER THAT GRAPH and cannot see the graph itself being too small. Demonstrated rather than argued: narrowing the alignment band by one doubling step yields a DAG retaining 3 cells where the true one retains 7 on `AC -> CA`, and that oracle PASSES, because a uniformly shrunken DAG is internally consistent. It catches asymmetric damage and is blind to uniform shrinkage. The cell-vs-column distinction this record turns on does not rest on it -- `GACA -> AGAT` is worked above from the alignments themselves -- so the ruling is unaffected; what is withdrawn is any reading of that clause as independent verification of the DAG.

THE WORKED CASE THAT MADE THIS URGENT, and the reason it is recorded rather than left in a session log. `CAG -> AGA` is EQUAL LENGTH, and equal length does NOT imply no indel: position-wise: C->A, A->G, G->A cost 3 del C, A=A, G=G, ins A cost 2 <- minimal Every cost-2 alignment matches ref offsets 1 and 2, so those bases ARE unchanged, the two changes ARE separated by them, and `general.md:33` describes them individually. `delins.md:16` has no antecedent here because under the minimal alignment there are no two CONSECUTIVE CHANGED nucleotides. `substitution.md:32` does not reach it either: its own example `GC -> TT` is one where two substitutions IS the minimal alignment, so the decomposition it marks `class="invalid"` was never a minimal one.

WHAT THIS RULES OUT, stated because it is the reading two independent agents reached on 2026-08-14 before finding this record's evidence: that the separation in `g.[261del;263_264insA]` is an ARTIFACT of choosing a del+ins decomposition, so `delins.md:16` fires and the split is a rule-1 violation of `substitution.md:32`. It is not an artifact. It is what the minimal alignment says, and the position-wise pairing that makes it look like an artifact is the thing with no authority behind it.

THE CODE PREMISE THIS FALSIFIES. `src/normalize/merge.rs` justifies three separate equal-length exemptions with the claim that an equal-length block 'has no gap to place, so there is no search' and therefore 'every matched base is a genuine coordinate-wise identity'. That premise is FALSE — a balanced del+ins pair is an equal-length block with two gaps — and all three guards key on `reference.len() != result.len()`, so each exempts precisely the class that breaks it. `FERRO_PARTITION=live` reaches the position-wise answer on this shape for that reason; the canonical family, which searches, does not.

SCOPE, MEASURED 2026-08-14 (#1939). The affected class is every equal-length block whose minimal alignment is cheaper than the position-wise one, i.e. any balanced del+ins pair; `CAG -> AGA` is the smallest instance. THE CLASS IS LARGE and grows with block length. Over equal-length uniform-random `ACGT` pairs, the rate with edit distance strictly below Hamming distance is 0.00% at L=2 (impossible), 4.98% at L=3, 11.98% at L=4, 20.05% at L=5 and 28.34% at L=6 — those five exhaustive — then 43.98%, 57.22% and 67.60% at L=8, 10 and 12 on 200,000 sampled pairs each. Past L~9 it is the MAJORITY of pairs, so this record governs a common shape and not a corner. THE SHIPPED EXPOSURE IS NIL. 840 cases drawn conditioned on edit < Hamming — 120 per length at L in {3,4,5,6,8,10,12}, each one `delins` over one block on its own 256-base-padded synthetic contig — produced indel-bearing members 840 times out of 840 and a lone spanning `delins` zero times: the default arm already reads unchanged-ness the way this record requires, so nothing here asks for a behaviour change. THE RESIDUAL IS ENTIRELY `FERRO_PARTITION=live`, which keeps the block whole and never discovers the indel, and which this record already names as giving the other answer. Its disagreement is LARGEST AT SHORT LENGTHS — 110/120 at L=3 falling monotonically to 14/120 at L=12 — the opposite of what a length-gated intuition predicts, and worth knowing before anyone gates work here on block size. WHAT IS STILL NOT MEASURED, stated so the figures above are not read wider than they were taken: the `g.` axis only, single-member inputs only, uniform-random composition, and no block near a window edge; and 'indel-bearing' is NOT 'verified minimal' — it says no sampled output took either shape nameable as wrong, not that each output equals THE minimal alignment, which would need an independent aligner and is the check worth writing if this is ever pinned as a guard. Read the counts as one spanning block versus a partition that places a gap, NOT as position-wise versus minimal: the naive one-substitution-per-differing-column spelling was emitted by neither arm, its bucket matching zero of the 840 cases on both. The property to key a generator on is edit distance against block length, not block length alone — a corpus that varies only length cannot vary this.

WHY IT IS NOT A MINIMALITY PRINCIPLE. `basics.md:38` omits minimality from the spec's design values, and this record does not smuggle it back in: minimal alignment is used to decide which BASES ARE UNCHANGED, a factual question about the sequences, not to prefer a shorter DESCRIPTION. `rulings[derivation-may-not-be-bounded-by-the-inputs-spelling]` and `rulings[canonical-form-choice-when-both-legal]` continue to govern which of several legal descriptions ships.

NO `applies_to` ROWS, deliberately. The two reproducers are synthetic (`NC_TEST.1:g.[260_261del;262_263insA;264delinsAC]` and its `n.` twin), not spec-harvested, and the generator refuses an `applies_to` naming a non-fixture row. They are pinned as ordinary tests instead: `normalize_reparse_invariant::a_del_beside_a_dup_re_spells_instead_of_colliding` and `issue_1284_transcript_axis_collision::a_noncoding_del_dup_collision_is_repaired`, both of which carry this record's reasoning in full.

THE COST MODEL IS NOT STATED BY THIS RECORD, AND TWO CANDIDATES DISAGREE — RECORDED 2026-08-14, NOT RESOLVED. 'Minimal' presupposes a cost model and this record never names one. Two are in play: substitution as a single unit-cost operation (Levenshtein), and substitution as a delete plus an insert (cost 2). BOTH of this record's own worked examples are silent on the difference — `CAG -> AGA` and `GACA -> AGAT` are unaffected — but the models DIVERGE on a case this repository already pins. On `ATGC -> GCTG` (the `1420-v4` block, `TEMPLATE` 21-24): unit-cost substitution gives cost 3, two minimal alignments, unchanged `{22,23}`; substitution-as-two gives cost 4, six minimal alignments, unchanged `{23}` alone. So the record decides a different set of bases depending on a parameter it does not fix. TWO PIECES OF EVIDENCE POINT AT UNIT-COST SUBSTITUTION, and neither is a ruling. This record's own framing of `CAG -> AGA` calls the position-wise reading 'cost 3', which is only true if a substitution costs 1. And `issue_1539_split_member_separation::forced_unchanged_columns` — the closed-form detector this record names as its own origin — hardcodes unit-cost substitution. WHAT IS OWED: an operator ruling naming the model, at which point `1420-v4`'s unchanged set follows rather than being chosen. Until then, do not cite this record for a case where the two models differ without saying which you used. Both are enumerated and pinned in `tests/it/common/minimal_alignment.rs`, so the question is now measurable rather than arguable.

RE-GROUNDED AS A HOUSE CHOICE, 2026-08-15. THE RULING IS UNCHANGED; ONLY ITS AUTHORITY IS. This record named `general.md:33` as GOVERNING, which claimed the recommendations decide how a reference base is judged unchanged. They do not. The clause raises the question — it keys on variants 'separated by one or more nucleotides' — and supplies no procedure for answering it, so citing it as authority for the minimal-alignment reading asserted a warrant the text does not carry. The record now carries a `house_choice` under `README.md` rule 5's silent limb instead, names no governing clause, and is NEVER citable as conformance. The four clauses stay cited, because 'clause-free' means no governing clause and not no citations: what was read, and why none of it reaches, is the substance of `considered_and_rejected`.

WHAT PROMPTED IT, AND WHY THAT ROUTE MATTERS. A spec-only review of the pinned submodule — restricted to `assets/hgvs-nomenclature`, with this ledger and the repository's own prose deliberately out of scope, precisely so it could not absorb the framing it was asked to test — reported that neither this reading nor the position-wise one it displaced has textual support, and that the one on-point passage points the other way. The counts are in `considered_and_rejected`. It is worth recording that the review was commissioned to test THIS record's replacement premise, having just been used to refute the sibling record's; the answer came back against the commissioner's interest, which is the reason to trust it.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT CHANGE, STATED SO THE SCOPE IS NOT READ WIDER THAN IT IS. The ruling itself — a reference base is unchanged iff every minimal alignment matches it — stands, and stands as the project's own rule rather than the spec's. Both guards are untouched and still pass. No output moves: #1939 measured shipped exposure at NIL, 840 of 840 sampled cases already producing indel-bearing members under the default arm, so this is a change to what the ledger CLAIMS and not to what ferro emits. `rulings[derivation-may-not-be-bounded-by-the-inputs-spelling]`'s correction, which uses this record as its falsifier, is unaffected: that correction establishes that the position-wise ground is false, which remains true whatever grounds this reading.

THE ALTERNATIVE CONSIDERED FOR THE RECORD ITSELF, NOT ONLY FOR THE RULE. Re-opening this as `undecided` was weighed and rejected. It would un-ground behaviour that ships, the two committed guards, and the sibling record's correction, in exchange for describing as unsettled a question the project has in fact settled. A house choice is the honest state: the choice was made, it is ours, and it is not the spec's.

#### `unequal-length-block-a-placed-gap-is-not-a-separation`

**Status:** decided

**The question.** On an unequal-length changed block, ferro's partitioner cuts at the one column where a payload base coincides with a reference base and emits the residual gap as a member of its own. Do `general.md:34` / `DNA/delins.md:17` govern the members that cut produces, or does `DNA/delins.md:47` recommend the spanning `delins`?

**Governing clause.**

- `docs/recommendations/DNA/delins.md:47`
  > **The "delins" format is recommended**

**Also cited.**

- `docs/recommendations/DNA/delins.md:46`
  > parts of the inserted sequence "align" with the reference sequence, giving an alternative description like `c.[850_869del;874_881del;887_897del;901_902insG]`
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/delins.md:17`
  > two variants separated by one or more nucleotides should be described individually and **not** as a "delins"
- `docs/recommendations/general.md:33`
  > two variants separated by one or more nucleotides should be described individually and **not** as a "delins"
- `docs/consultation/open-issues.md:77-78`
  > one variant can be described using different formats. This is undesired; HGVS recommendations should be extended by specifying when to use which format.

**The ruling.**

OPERATOR RULING, 2026-08-14, AMENDED 2026-08-15 - DECIDED for `:47`, at the PARTITIONER, and SCOPED TO THE CODING DNA AXIS. Filed as #1610 against `NM_TEST.1:n.2_5delinsAAC`, which returned `n.[2_3delinsAA;9del]`: four reference bases (`CGCG`) against a three-base payload (`AAC`), cut at the one column where the payload's own `C` lands on a reference `C`, with the residual deletion 3'-shifted seven bases down a `G` run. The output asserts two variants far apart where the sequences determine no such separation.

READ THE SCOPE BEFORE CITING THIS RECORD: THE ISSUE'S OWN REPRODUCTION IS NOT CLOSED BY IT. The rule is gated on `CoincidenceCarveOut::may_disbelieve_a_separation`, so it runs on `c.` and nowhere else. The reproduction above is spelled `n.`, which is outside that scope, so `NM_TEST.1:n.2_5delinsAAC` still returns `n.[2_3delinsAA;9del]`. What this ruling closes is the same block on the coding axis. The `n.`/`g.` half of #1610 remains OPEN and is pinned as open by `issue_1610_lone_unequal_length_delins::a_lone_unequal_length_delins_is_not_split`, which asserts the defect rather than the fix.

THE GROUND. `general.md:33` and `DNA/delins.md:17` are conditionals whose antecedent is "two variants separated by one or more nucleotides". On an unequal-length block that antecedent is not established - it is manufactured by whichever alignment the partitioner picked. `README.md`'s ruleset states the general fact, in the list of reasons rules 2 and 3 are best effort: "The variant's decomposition is not recoverable. Recovering one means *choosing* an alignment, and the spec does not say which, so there is no derivable form to converge on." What remains is a `README.md` rule 6 choice among conformant forms. `DNA/delins.md:46` describes the construction exactly and `:47` states the spec's own preference for the span. NEITHER FORM IS FORBIDDEN AND THIS MUST NEVER BE CITED AS CONFORMANCE.

A QUOTE THIS RECORD USED TO CARRY HAS BEEN WITHDRAWN AS FALSE. The 2026-08-14 text argued from a second `README.md` sentence - "For an equal-length block the column correspondence is unique, so 'two variants separated by N nucleotides' is a fact about the variant" - and contrasted it with the unequal-length case. #1937 DELETED that sentence from `README.md` as false, on the authority of `unchanged-is-read-over-every-minimal-alignment`: `CAG -> AGA` is equal length with edit distance 2, not the position-wise 3, so an equal-length block's correspondence need not be unique either. Do not restate it. The ruling does not depend on it - the unequal-length half stands on its own - but the LENGTH GATE's ground is condition 1's decided direction scope, not a uniqueness contrast.

IT IS NOT SPELLING-RELATIVE, AND THE ISSUE'S OWN PHRASING IS. #1610 argues that "`general.md:34` does not reach it, because `:34` speaks of 'two variants separated by' and the INPUT asserts one". Read literally that is a rule keyed on how the submitter wrote it, which `canonical-form-choice-when-both-legal` (decided) excludes outright. The ruling here is the spelling-independent form of the same argument: the antecedent fails because the SEQUENCES do not determine a separation. Two spellings of one variant derive the same block and reach the same verdict.

THE FOUR CONDITIONS, each with the negative control that fixes it. (1) NET DELETION - the direction scope of `delins-merge-vs-individual-gap-two-or-more` (decided 2026-08-11), which reaches a payload shorter than its span and does not reach net insertions; it also keeps #422 and #999 outside this rule entirely. (2) EVERY SEPARATION EXACTLY ONE UNCHANGED BASE - the regime the sibling collapse (`split_buys_no_higher_priority_type`) already runs in; real distance between members is `general.md:34`'s population and stays split. (3) SOME MEMBER SUPPLIES BASES - `:46`'s mechanism is an INSERTED SEQUENCE re-aligning, so a split that inserts nothing cannot be its artefact; that is `delins-recommendation-reach-when-the-input-arrives-split` (decided), and W58's `c.[992_993del;995_997del;999_1004del]` stays split. (4) EVERY OTHER MEMBER RENDERS AS A `delins` - a substitution, an inversion or a duplication is a real type the split buys. Separation is read off the DERIVED partition throughout, per `separation-is-a-property-of-the-spelling-not-of-the-variant`.

WHAT IS NEW IS ONLY CONDITION 4's TREATMENT OF A PURE DELETION. `partition_block` already collapsed this regime when EVERY member rendered as a `delins`; it declined as soon as one member was a pure deletion, on the ground that a `del` is a higher-priority type. That reading treats the deletion as a variant the sequences separated. On an unequal-length block it is the gap the aligner PLACED, and where it lands is the same choice that manufactured the separation. `:46`'s own published alternative, `c.[850_869del;874_881del;887_897del;901_902insG]`, is mostly `del` members, and `:47` recommends the span over precisely that.

THE AXIS SCOPE, AND WHY IT IS THE RULING RATHER THAN THE COUNTERWEIGHT. The 2026-08-14 text shipped this rule AXIS-BLIND and reconciled that with `delins-payload-coincidence-carve-out-is-coding-dna-scoped` by arguing the latter governs only the terminal COALESCE passes, one level below the partitioner. That reconciliation is WITHDRAWN, for a measured reason rather than a textual one. #1899 had already scoped the sibling collapse INSIDE `partition_block` to the coding DNA axis, threading `CoincidenceCarveOut` through it - so the partitioner was already carrying that scope, and this rule sat directly AFTER that gate re-admitting on `g.`/`n.` exactly what it excludes. Measured, that took `spec_conformance_axis`'s `guard_violations` from 0 to 5, all of them the REJECTED SVD-WG010 shape on the frameless axes: `NC_TEST.1:g.[265_266delinsAT;268_269del] -> g.265_269delinsATA` and `NR_TEST.1:n.[36_39del;41T>A] -> n.36_41delinsTA`. A rank-1 conformance regression outranks a rule 6 preference, so the rule takes the gate. `:47`'s stated ground - preventing "incorrect predictions for the consequences on protein level" - has nothing to bite on where no protein is coded, which is the same reason that record gives.

WHAT THE GATE COSTS, STATED PLAINLY. The MSH2 GRCh37 row `NC_000002.11:g.47639670_47639673delinsTT` does NOT move and keeps its split; `delins_equal_vs_unequal_length_discriminator`'s negative guard against the spanning form is NOT retired and stands as written. An earlier revision of this record said the opposite of both. `delins_equal_vs_unequal_length_hermetic`'s `g.` arm is likewise untouched, now for two independent reasons - its derived split carries a lone substitution (condition 4) AND its axis is out of scope.

THE COST, RE-MEASURED AT BASE `d055fc10` WITH THE PREPARED REFERENCE ARMED (`FERRO_MANIFEST` set; an unarmed run early-returns and reports green). NO CONFLUENCE IS LOST: `spec_conformance_axis` `converged` holds at 11 016 (3') and 10 755 (5'), every `split_*` bucket holds, and `multi_member_cis_axis::axis_normalized` passes unchanged with its row set intact. Every rank-1 figure is unchanged, `guard_violations` included at 0. The ONE census figure that moves is the coding-axis merge instrument, `coding_axis_separation_two_or_more_merges` 3 -> 5 in both directions, re-pinned and adjudicated row by row in `spec_conformance_axis`'s module docs: s01-c3m-pair-delins-del-p4-sep3 and s01-c3p-pair-delins-del-p4-sep3 (corpus row ids, not record ids), one variant in both shuffle directions, `NM_TEST.1:c.[24_27delinsGTCA;31_34del] -> c.24_34delinsGTCATTC`. Those rows are within the population `coding-axis-merges-are-a-disclosed-general-34-deviation` (decided 2026-08-13) governs, and the clause carrying the rise is `DNA/delins.md:47` via this record.

THE 2026-08-14 COST FIGURES ARE SUPERSEDED AND MUST NOT BE QUOTED. That text recorded `converged` 11 016 -> 10 959 and 10 753 -> 10 696, and attributed the fall to `canonicalize_from_sequence`'s input-relative weight bound with the prediction that disabling the bound would return both. #1899 DELETED that bound, and the prediction is confirmed - the fall does not occur at all on the current base.

REPRESENTATION EFFECT, re-measured at the same base over `examples/dump_normalized_corpus.rs` with the generator copied into the base checkout so both dumps cover the same 95 614 rows. 1 798 rows move (1.9%), of which 776 WERE PREVIOUSLY FIXED POINTS - a real migration for a consumer keying on the normalized string. EVERY MOVED ROW IS ON A CODING AXIS (`c.` 1 283, `cx.` 515; `g.` 0, `n.` 0, and 0 on the protein families), which is the axis scope visible in the corpus. The migrating 776 split `c.` 550 / `cx.` 226 and are all in `lone_net_deletion_delins`. 1 797 of the moved rows are that family collapsing onto the span; the remaining 1 is one `del_plus_sub` row, `NM_TEST.1:c.[10_11del;13T>A]`, whose output moves from `c.10_*1delinsAATTT` to `c.10_13delinsAA` and which was not a fixed point before. Every other family reads 0.

THE ZEROS ARE MEASURED, NOT STRUCTURAL, AND THE FAMILY IS NEW. Before `lone_net_deletion_delins` the corpus could not build a lone unequal-length NET DELETION `delins` at all - its only lone spanning family, `split_vs_spanning_delins`, is a net insertion, and its other lone `delins` families are equal-length - so this rule would have measured a guaranteed structural zero, with DIRECTION rather than shape the missing dimension. The controls are populated and genuinely read 0: `split_vs_spanning_delins` (9 216 rows, net insertion) and `delins_hiding_an_inversion` (8 640 rows, equal length). The `g.` zero is likewise measured rather than structural - the family is drawn on `g.`, `c.` and `cx.`, and the `g.` rows are present and unmoved.

THE COUNTERWEIGHT, RECORDED UNANSWERED. The 2026-08-14 text named the axis gate as the live alternative and predicted its cost - "if that reading is adopted, this rule takes an axis gate and the MSH2 row goes back to its split". It was adopted, and that is what happened. What is now unanswered is the other side: `:47`'s FIRST ground is simplicity, which is axis-neutral, and a reader who weighted it would say the `n.` and `g.` halves of #1610 are still defects. This record does not answer them; it declines to buy them at the price of a rank-1 regression. Closing them means either showing those five SVD-WG010 rows are acceptable, or finding a discriminator that separates them from #1610's shape.

#### `whole-span-reverse-complement-types-as-inv`

**Status:** decided

**The question.** A span whose whole content is replaced by its exact reverse complement always admits a competing partition at the columns where the complement coincides with the reference. `inversion-vs-two-delins-76-83` typed the span `inv` because that partition was two `delins`, which `general.md:55` does not rank; #1230 split the span because that partition was substitutions, which `:55` ranks above inversion. Does the TYPE of the competing partition decide the typing, or does the span?

**Applies to.** `c.76_83inv`

**Governing clause.**

- `docs/recommendations/DNA/inversion.md:5`
  > a sequence change where, compared to a reference sequence, **more than one nucleotide** replacing the original sequence is the reverse complement of the original sequence

**Also cited.**

- `docs/recommendations/DNA/inversion.md:16`
  > a one-nucleotide inversion should be described as a [substitution](substitution.md)
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/inversion.md:33-34`
  > **`NM_004006.2:c.4145_4160inv`**<br> inversion of the 16 nucleotides from position `c.4145` to `c.4160`.
- `docs/recommendations/general.md:33`
  > two variants separated by one or more nucleotides should be described individually and **not** as a "delins"
- `docs/recommendations/general.md:55`
  > the preferred description is: (1) substitution, (2) deletion, (3) inversion, (4) duplication, (5) insertion
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/delins.md:5`
  > one or more nucleotides are replaced by one or more other nucleotides **and which is not** a substitution or inversion
- `docs/recommendations/DNA/substitution.md:32`
  > changes involving two or more consecutive nucleotides are described as deletion-insertion (delins) so the description <code class="invalid">c.[79G>T;80C>T]</code> is not correct
- `docs/recommendations/style.md:9`
  > are to be interpreted as described in [RFC 2119]

**The ruling.**

OPERATOR RULING, 2026-08-13 — TYPE THE SPAN, NOT THE COMPETITOR. A span whose whole content is replaced by its exact reverse complement is typed `inv`, uniformly: however much of the interior coincides with the reference, and whatever the competing partition is made of. The test is `block == revcomp(block)` over the trimmed block, evaluated on the resulting sequence, and it settles the typing before any cut is considered. `inversion.md:5` governs, being the clause that states the property — and it states it over the whole span.

OVERTURNED. #1230 (CLOSED) held that a substitution competitor splits the span, because `general.md:55` ranks substitution (1) above inversion (3). `inversion-vs-two-delins-76-83` (`decided`) held that a `delins` competitor does not, because `:55` ranks no `delins` — adding that #1230's case "is untouched and still splits". The competitor-type distinction is overturned. #1230's outcome falls; `inversion-vs-two-delins-76-83`'s outcome stands but its reason is superseded, and that record is amended here rather than left asserting the distinction. Per the repository `CLAUDE.md`, #1230's body is agent-drafted under @msto's account, so no maintainer position is reversed.

GROUND 1, THE STRONGEST: THE DISTINCTION RESTS ON A CLAUSE A DECIDED RECORD ALREADY SCOPED OUT. Both halves of the old rule argue about `general.md:55` — #1230 applying it, `inversion-vs-two-delins-76-83` declining it. But `adjudication-precedence-order`'s escalation E1 (`decided`) holds that `:55` ranks single-variant type labels for one span, never a multi-member allele against a spanning description, and so "cannot settle a merge-versus-split question at all". Whole-span `inv` against a partition is that question. A rule whose whole content is which side `:55` reaches cannot survive a reading the ledger already carries.

GROUND 2: THE CLAUSE IS STATED OVER THE SPAN. "more than one nucleotide replacing the original sequence is the reverse complement of the original sequence" is a predicate over the replaced run as a whole; interior coincidence is not a term in it, and is uninformative anyway — complementation is an involution, so column `i` coincides exactly when its mirror `L-1-i` does. `general.md:33` is stated over "two variants", and a span that is one inversion is one variant; `separation-rule-force-modal-or-negation` grades `:33` as rule 2 (a preference), so it could not compel a split even where it reached. `substitution.md:32` marks the split spelling of CONSECUTIVE changes `class="invalid"` — a prohibition on adjacency, not a requirement to split at a gap. `inversion.md:16` redirects the degenerate one-nucleotide case to a substitution: a floor on length, not a rule about interiors.

GROUND 3: THE SPEC'S OWN PUBLISHED INVERSION HAS THIS SHAPE. `inversion.md:33-34` publishes `NM_004006.2:c.4145_4160inv` and prints no bases. Derived from the accession the spec names (the prepared reference's supplemental FASTA, not the `LRG_199t1` proxy used earlier — byte-identical at 13,993 nt), CDS start derived as the longest ORF (position 245, `c.1_3 == ATG`, 3,685 aa): `c.4145_4160` is `TCCAGGAGTCCCTCAC`, revcomp `GTGAGGGACTCCTGGA`, coinciding at 0-based columns 3-5 and 10-12 — two three-base unchanged interior runs, 10 of 16 columns changed. The spec's own worked inversion is one the old rule would have had to argue about, and the spec simply calls it `inv`. Worth precisely: the example is the spec's, the bases are derived — stronger than spec-silence, weaker than a verbatim statement.

REGISTER: RULE 6, NOT RULE 1. #1703 established that `inversion.md:5` makes a span QUALIFY while no clause COMPELS the description — every imperative mention of `inv` is a prohibition or redirect (`inversion.md:16`, `duplication.md:20`, `insertion.md:18`, `inversion.md:23`, `protein/delins.md:60`), and the three positive imperatives govern the `inv` token inside an `ins` payload. `delins.md:5`'s "and which is not a substitution or inversion" excludes a delins from being an inversion; it does not reach a multi-member allele, which is what the competitors are. The split forms are conformant, so this is `README.md` rule 6 — "among multiple conformant forms: the maintainers choose" — with rule 7 disclosure. Citing it as a rule 1 correction misstates what was decided and what it costs.

CORROBORATION, NOT AUTHORITY. `adjudication-precedence-order` records why Mutalyzer is not an oracle here. Probed live, spanning both competitor types: 14 of 14 descriptions across four loci normalize to the whole-span `inv` — substitution competitors `NM_000500.9:c.[710T>A;713T>A]` (#1541's row), `NC_000017.11:g.[43044327G>T;43044329T>A;43044331A>C]` (#1703's row) and four `NM_004006.2` sweep pairs; `delins` competitors `c.[76_77delinsTG;82_83delinsTT]` plus two sweep rows. No competitor-type distinction. The earlier record's "26 of 26 probes" was not re-run and is NOT current.

COST, RE-MEASURED ON `origin/main` @7cc9846c. (1) #1541 moves: its issue text calls both spellings fixed points, which is stale — all three converge today on the split (`inv` and `delinsACGA` both report `changed = true`), and this ruling moves that form to `c.710_713inv`. Bases re-derived: `c.710_713` is `TCGT`, revcomp `ACGA`, interior `CG` self-complementary. (2) #1575's authored inversions stop being repartitioned, and THE POPULATION IS 93, NOT THE 155 THIS RECORD WAS FIRST COSTED ON, because #1706 landed in between. At @7cc9846c, 155 of 2,075 rows repartitioned — 87 into substitutions only, 43 into an `inv` core with flanking substitutions, 25 into member sets containing a `delins`. #1706 (`64fc76df`) added a post-hoc inversion run scan absorbing 62 of them (all 43 `inv`-core, 19 of the 25 delins-bearing), leaving 93 on the `live` arm; #1835's flip of the default partition rule to `canonical-coalesced` then took one more, so `CENSUS_REPARTITIONED` pins 92. Read the count off that constant, not off prose. Both figures are ALL the all-substitutions case — exactly the population this ruling overturns and the one #1575 did not ask about. Six of them still RENDER a delins member: that is the coding-codon carve-out merging two isolated substitutions after the partition, not a delins piece — count by pieces, never by rendered members. Both figures re-derived from the committed pin `tests/fixtures/inversion-sweep/cases.tsv` with one instrument, reproducing the 87/43/25 split as its control. DO NOT QUOTE 155 AS CURRENT. (3) #1230's checked-in guards must be flipped by the implementer. (4) Five `.rs` prose sites asserted the overturned reason and are corrected here, because one rule written in several places and left to drift is this project's named recurring failure: `inversion_sweep.rs`'s module doc and `CENSUS_REPARTITIONED`, `src/conformance/inversion_sweep.rs`'s `Repartitioned` doc, `case_harvest.rs`'s `CYP21A2_TARGET` doc and `the_inversion_preference_control_still_holds` (doc and assertion message — the latter having told #1541's implementer that a uniform fix was the error to avoid), and `spec_worked_example_rules.rs`'s W15.1 rationale. No pinned value or expected string moves. The same stale reasoning in `tests/fixtures/case-harvest/cases.json`'s `note` fields is left alone: blessed conformance inputs, a separate reviewable change.

TWO EARLIER COSTINGS ARE WITHDRAWN, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS (R6). `inversion-vs-two-delins-76-83` costed the unconditional `inv` at "moves ONE corpus row", and reported the gated implementation's blast radius as a STRUCTURAL zero because "the only family that builds a reverse-complement block emits its pieces at separation 0". Neither is current. `examples/dump_normalized_corpus.rs` has since gained `separated_revcomp_runs` (separations 0, 1, 2, 4, 8), so the corpus is no longer blind to SEPARATION — but it remains blind to RUN WIDTH, since `REVCOMP_RUN_WIDTH` is a constant `2` and the one-column run was excluded deliberately, so no LONE SUBSTITUTION competitor can arise there. `long_block_inversion` can: `near_palindromic_core` perturbs two positions of an exact palindrome, each costing two differences under reverse complement — re-derived as 0-based {10, 504, 519, 1013} at length 1024 and {10, 542, 557, 1089} at 1100, every run one column wide. The property is variable in the corpus and the rows are named. NO A/B WAS RUN: measuring it needs the normalizer change this record deliberately does not make, so the figure is owed by the implementing change.

THE 457 nt ROW: THE GUARD FAILURE IS REAL, THE ATTRIBUTION IS NOT. #1703 records that `case_harvest::no_emitted_output_puts_two_members_on_consecutive_nucleotides` reports 12 violations from `NC_000013.10:g.100809575_100810031inv`, calling it a rule 1 violation of `substitution.md:32`. Re-run at @7cc9846c under `FERRO_PARTITION=canonical-coalesced`, the guard does fail with exactly 12 pairs at the listed coordinates — but `live` and `shadow` return the row unchanged, while `canonical` and `canonical-coalesced` return an identical 133-member allele (47 delins, 44 substitutions, 18 del, 16 ins, 4 inv, 2 dup, 2 repeat) in which ALL 12 pairs involve an `ins` member. An `ins` consumes no reference, so its coordinates flank an insertion point rather than occupying bases: ZERO pairs are two substitutions, the minimum gap between reference-consuming members is 1, and `substitution.md:32`'s named `class="invalid"` shape occurs nowhere in the row. (The instrument was controlled against `:32`'s own invalid example, which it scores as one adjacent pair.) So the question is whether `member_bounds` should treat an `ins` junction as occupying its flanking coordinates — a reading the guard encodes rather than argues. THIS RULING DOES NOT DECIDE THAT and need not: it stands on rule 6, and fixing the typing closes the failure anyway, the row returning to a single `inv`. Do not repeat unqualified "12 pairs of members on consecutive nucleotides, which `substitution.md:32` marks invalid by name" — the count is right, the attribution is not.

THE COUNTERWEIGHT, UNANSWERED. `general.md:55` does rank substitution above inversion, and what retires it is a SCOPE argument (E1's), not a refutation of the ranking; a reader who held that `:55` reaches a partition would reach #1230's answer without contradicting the spec's text. It was rejected because a decided record already scopes `:55`, and because `inversion.md:33-34` comes out wrong under it. Second: scale. The uniform rule types a 457 nt span with four changed columns as one `inv` exactly as it types a 4 nt span, and supplies no length or density bound. The obvious bound was already refuted by measurement — unchanged columns under a random inversion are `2 * Binom(floor(n/2), 1/4)`, so a coincidence FRACTION discriminates only as a proxy for `n`. A later bound would be a new ruling and must say so.

SUBSUMES `inversion-vs-a-mixed-member-competitor`, WHICH LANDED FIRST (with #1706, `64fc76df`) WHILE THIS RECORD WAS OPEN — so the reconciliation is made here, not owed to a future merge. The relationship is subsumption, not conflict: that record types the `inv` where the competitor mixes lone substitutions with multi-column members, and says in terms that it disturbs neither `inversion-vs-two-delins-76-83` nor #1230's all-substitutions case, "which BOTH gates refuse". This ruling reaches all of those and the one they exclude. So: (a) the narrower record is NOT a live carve-out — its outcome is a special case of this one; (b) its clause (4), preserving #1230's split, is superseded exactly as `inversion-vs-two-delins-76-83`'s equivalent sentence is, and this change appends that supersession, moving neither outcome nor any pin; (c) its implementation widens a gate from `no_piece_is_a_lone_substitution` to `not_every_piece_is_a_lone_substitution`, which this ruling retires altogether — the uniform rule needs no piece-type predicate, so the widened gate becomes dead rather than wrong. None of this touches #1706's post-hoc run scan, a separate mechanism not adjudicated here.

THREE CONVERGENCES, REACHED BY TWO SESSIONS THAT COULD NOT SEE EACH OTHER. Register: #1706 rules explicitly as a rule 6 choice and warns against citing it as conformance ("the competing form violates nothing") — the same classification reached here and by #1703, three times over; it also corroborates the correction above, a record whose central example IS the 457 nt row declining to call its output a rule 1 violation. Clause: both decline `general.md:55`, #1706 grounding it on `conflicting-member-geometry-refusal-scope` — which cites `:55` precisely to record that it does not reach a multi-member allele, "this repository's recorded cautionary error" — where this record grounds it on E1; take the pair as the settled position on `:55`. Measurement: #1706 measures that row's canonical partition at 133 pieces whose first is `g.100809575A>T`; the independent re-measurement here returns a 133-member allele whose first member is `100809575A>T`. Two instruments, one row. #1706's argument on it generalises: refusing the `inv` makes the notation turn on whether one interior column happens to be self-complementary, "a property of the SEQUENCE, not of the EVENT" — an instability WORST in the all-substitutions case, where a single coincidental column is the whole difference between one `inv` and a multi-member allele. That is the strongest statement of why the competitor-type distinction had to go, and this ledger owes it to #1706.

NOT DISTURBED, NOT IMPLEMENTED. Nothing about WHERE a whole-span reverse complement's boundaries fall — trimming, shifting and hull selection are untouched — and nothing about spans that are not exact reverse complements, where the competing partition governs unchanged. It does not reach `delins-adjacent-members-when-both-consume-reference`, `duplication-must-ranks-the-label-not-the-partition`, or any other typing question. It ships no code: the normalizer is unchanged, so it moves no output by itself. The moves it decides are #1703's, #1541's and #1575's, together with the `Representation-Change:` measurement over a real corpus that this record does not supply.
